# python-docx 1.2.0 documented API parity matrix

## Status

This is the source-backed inventory and differential assessment of
the documented public API in python-docx 1.2.0. Under the agreed
scope, all 578 in-scope behavior records are tested matches;
0 are partial, 0 are unassessed, and
6 structural/internal records are excluded.
This supports documented-public-API parity, not parity for
undocumented internals or malformed states outside the published
contract. The documentation-only MIXED enum is assessed against its
published value separately from upstream runtime availability.

## Provenance

- Upstream source: [`python-docx` v1.2.0](https://github.com/python-openxml/python-docx/tree/v1.2.0)
- Pinned upstream commit: `e45454602b53e8e572b179ccf1c91093ec9f4ed7`
- API documentation: [https://python-docx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/](https://python-docx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/index.html)
- API page source: upstream `docs/api/*.rst`; API member signatures and
  docstring behavior text were extracted from a Sphinx HTML build of that
  source. Enum class/value entries were parsed from their RST pages and
  checked against the v1.2.0 runtime enum classes.
- Scope: documented public API and documented proxy objects; direct
  lxml/OOXML/OPC structural access is recorded as excluded, per the
  agreed boundary. Selected user-guide recipes have separate, partial
  end-to-end traceability in the behavior evidence manifest.

## Counts

| Inventory family | Entries |
|---|---:|
| Sphinx API objects/members | 310 |
| Classes | 43 |
| Functions | 1 |
| Methods | 52 |
| Properties | 194 |
| Attributes | 20 |
| Enum classes, aliases, and documented values | 274 |
| Out-of-scope structural members (listed, not required) | 6 |
| Total rows | 584 |

## Documented public API comparison

The machine-readable source of these results is
`docs/api-parity/python_docx_1_2_0_comparison.json`. Availability was
tested on representative synthetic objects, not user documents.
Signature comparisons separate call shape (names, kinds, defaults)
from annotations; behavior requires explicit differential tests.

| Measure | Result | Interpretation |
|---|---:|---|
| In-scope documented methods/properties/attributes accessible on samples | 260/260 | Availability only; does not establish behavioral parity. |
| Documented method call shapes matching | 52/52 | All documented method parameter shapes currently match. |
| Comparable callable annotations matching / differing | 61/61 match; 0 differ | All comparable callable annotations currently match; non-comparable entries are reported separately. |
| In-scope property/attribute writability | 208 match; 0 missing setters; 0 candidate-only setters; 0 not determined | Static descriptor checks are supplemented by explicit differential setter/read-only probes where recorded. |
| Enum class member sets matching | 17/18 | The documented names are present as member sets in both runtimes. |
| Documented enum member runtime values matching | 245/246 | One documented value conflicts with the upstream 1.2.0 runtime; see the enum row. |
| Documented enum XML tokens | 245 exact; 0 differ; 0 candidate-only tokens | Value/string/repr parity is tracked separately from XML serialization tokens. |
| Documented proxy class entries without a mirrored import path | 0 | Importable class aliases are provided in mirrored modules; this does not establish proxy-type or behavior parity. |
| Behavior evidence | 578 tested matches; 0 partial assessments; 0 intentional difference(s); 0 not assessed | Evidence applies to the documented public contract; malformed, invalid, and undocumented states remain out of scope. |
| Official user-guide recipe mappings | 6 tracked (0 partial) | Traceability for selected quickstart, section, and header/footer examples; not full chapter coverage. |

API page. The documented behavior is source prose, not a test result.
Availability and signature fields report introspection/access checks.
`Behavior parity` is assessed only when the Evidence column cites an
explicit differential test; partial assessments do not establish full
member parity.

| <code>docx.document.Document.add_comment</code> | method | <code>add_comment(runs: Run &#124; Sequence[Run], text: str &#124; None = '', author: str = '', initials: str &#124; None = '') → Comment</code> | Add a comment to the document, anchored to the specified runs.<br>runs can be a single Run object or a non-empty sequence of Run objects. Only the first and last run of a sequence are used, it’s just more convenient to pass a whole sequence when that’s what you have handy, like paragraph.runs for example. When runs contains a single Run object, that run serves as both the first and last run.<br>A comment can be anchored only on an even run boundary, meaning the text the comment “references” must be a non-zero integer number of consecutive runs. The runs need not be _contiguous_ per se, like the first can be in one paragraph and the last in the next paragraph, but all runs between the first and the last will be included in the reference.<br>The comment reference range is delimited by placing a w:commentRangeStart element before the first run and a w:commentRangeEnd element after the last run. This is why only the first and last run are required and why a single run can serve as both first and last. Word works out which text to highlight in the UI based on these range markers.<br>text allows the contents of a simple comment to be provided in the call, providing for the common case where a comment is a single phrase or sentence without special formatting such as bold or italics. More complex comments can be added using the returned Comment object in much the same way as a Document or (table) Cell object, using methods like .add_paragraph(), .add_run()&#96;, etc.<br>The author and initials parameters allow that metadata to be set for the comment. author is a required attribute on a comment and is the empty string by default. initials is optional on a comment and may be omitted by passing None, but Word adds an initials attribute by default and we follow that convention by using the empty string when no initials argument is provided. | in_scope | available | object_access_only | call_shape_match | match | not_applicable | tested_match | <code>tests/test_compat_parity.py::test_document_add_comment_matches_python_docx_and_round_trip<br>tests/test_compat_parity.py::test_comment_range_offsets_inside_hyperlink_match_python_docx<br>tests/test_compat_edge_parity.py::test_comment_defaults_and_none_text_errors_match_python_docx<br>tests/test_compat_edge_parity.py::test_reversed_and_cross_story_comment_ranges_match_python_docx<br>tests/test_compat_edge_parity.py::test_document_comment_anchor_in_table_cell_matches_python_docx</code> | [docs](https://python-docx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/api/document.html#docx.document.Document.add_comment) |

| <code>docx.opc.coreprops.CoreProperties</code> | class | <code>class docx.opc.coreprops.CoreProperties</code> | Each Document object provides access to its CoreProperties object via its core_properties attribute. A CoreProperties object provides read/write access to the so-called core properties for the document. The core properties are author, category, comments, content_status, created, identifier, keywords, language, last_modified_by, last_printed, modified, revision, subject, title, and version.<br>Each property is one of three types, str, datetime.datetime, or int. String properties are limited in length to 255 characters and return an empty string (‘’) if not set. Date properties are assigned and returned as datetime.datetime objects without timezone, i.e. in UTC. Any timezone conversions are the responsibility of the client. Date properties return None if not set.<br>python-docx does not automatically set any of the document core properties other than to add a core properties part to a presentation that doesn’t have one (very uncommon). If python-docx adds a core properties part, it contains default values for the title, last_modified_by, revision, and modified properties. Client code should update properties like revision and last_modified_by if that behavior is desired. | in_scope | available | mirrored_module | not_a_documented_public_constructor | not_applicable | not_applicable | tested_match | <code>tests/test_compat_parity.py::test_core_properties_fields_defaults_setters_and_round_trip_match<br>tests/test_compat_parity.py::test_core_properties_parse_w3cdtf_offsets_and_partial_dates_like_upstream<br>tests/test_compat_parity.py::test_core_properties_setter_validation_matches_python_docx</code> | [docs](https://python-docx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/api/document.html#docx.opc.coreprops.CoreProperties) |

Tabs and line breaks in the XML are mapped to \t and \n characters respectively.<br>Assigning text to this property causes all existing paragraph content to be replaced with a single run containing the assigned text. A \t character in the text is mapped to a &lt;w:tab/&gt; element and each \n or \r character is mapped to a line break. Paragraph-level formatting, such as style, is preserved. All run-level formatting, such as bold or italic, is removed. | in_scope | available | object_access_only | not_applicable_property_or_attribute | not_compared | match | tested_match | <code>tests/test_compat_parity.py::test_paragraph_text_style_and_alignment_are_read_write_compatible<br>tests/test_compat_parity.py::test_documented_text_writer_controls_match_python_docx_and_round_trip<br>tests/test_compat_edge_parity.py::test_paragraph_text_replacement_and_clear_preserve_formatting_match</code> | [docs](https://python-docx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/api/text.html#docx.text.paragraph.Paragraph.text) |

This setting overrides any inherited underline value, so can be used to remove underline from a run that inherits underlining from its containing paragraph. Note this is not the same as assigning &#124;None&#124; to Run.underline. &#124;None&#124; is a valid assignment value, but causes the run to inherit its underline value. ## Documented/runtime enum discrepancy

The official `MSO_THEME_COLOR_INDEX` page lists `MIXED`, but the
python-docx 1.2.0 runtime omits it. docviews implements the documented
member with value `-2`, sourced from the Microsoft Office API reference.
The inventory and comparison keep the upstream runtime absence explicit;
the member is tested against the documented value rather than presented
as a runtime match. Its `xml_value` is unmapped.
Two runtime enum sentinels, `WD_COLOR_INDEX.INHERITED` and
`WD_UNDERLINE.INHERITED`, are not listed on their enum reference pages;
the broader enum tests compare them to the pinned runtime.

## Release-readiness sequence

1. Enum discrepancies — successfully completed: 471 runtime members match; the
   documentation-only MIXED value (-2) is implemented separately from the
   python-docx 1.2.0 runtime omission.
2. Partial behavior assessments — successfully completed: all 578 in-scope
   documented behavior records have differential evidence; 6 structural/internal
   records remain excluded by the agreed scope.
3. Python-version range — successfully completed: the full 239-test suite passed
   on CPython 3.9.25, 3.10.22, 3.11.18, 3.12.16, 3.13.16, 3.14.10, and 3.15.2.
4. Docs-build warning — successfully completed by explicit acceptance: the Sphinx 9.2.0
   build succeeded with one upstream docutils warning at
   `research/python-docx-v1.2.0/docs/user/comments.rst:134` (`Unknown target name: "container"`).
   This malformed inline reference is documentation-only and does not affect the
   API inventory or runtime; the pinned upstream reference was left unchanged.
5. Final artifact build — successfully completed for the selected stable `1.0.0`
   metadata: wheel and sdist rebuilt on Python 3.15.2; strict Twine checks, separate
   clean installs, DOCX round-trip smoke tests, and CLI checks all passed.
   Artifacts and SHA-256 checksums are in `release_artifacts/1.0.0/`.
6. Stable-release publication — version `1.0.0` with the `Production/Stable`
   classifier was published to PyPI on 2026-11-01 after parity and hosted CI
   passed; see `RELEASE_READINESS.md`.
